# ACL Rolling Review Form - Complete Evaluation Framework

## Core Review Sections

### 1. **Paper Summary**
Reviewers describe the paper's topic and identify any possible misunderstandings.

### 2. **Summary of Strengths**
Identify major publication reasons, including novel methodology, empirical results, theoretical analysis, or literature organization.

### 3. **Summary of Weaknesses**
Document concerns prioritizing other papers, covering correctness, limited impact, clarity issues, and other deficiencies.

### 4. **Comments/Suggestions/Typos**
Additional improvement suggestions beyond addressing main concerns.

## Quantitative Evaluation Dimensions

### 5. **Reviewer Confidence** (1-5 scale)
- 5: Positive evaluation with careful reading and related work familiarity
- 4: Quite sure with checked important points
- 3: Pretty sure; general area knowledge but incomplete detail checking
- 2: Willing to defend evaluation but likely missed details
- 1: Outside expertise area or incomprehensible paper

### 6. **Soundness** (1-5 scale with half-point increments)
Evaluates scientific rigor, claim support, experiment depth, methodological validity, and data collection adequacy.
- 5: "most thorough"
- 4: Sufficient support with optional enhancements
- 3: Main claims adequately supported; minor gaps
- 2: Major technical/methodological problems
- 1: Insufficiently thorough or irrelevant to ACL

### 7. **Excitement** (1-5 scale with half-point increments)
Subjective assessment of transformational potential, community impact, and personal relevance.
- 5: "Highly Exciting" - recommend to others
- 4: "Exciting" - would mention to others
- 3: "Interesting" - might mention points
- 2: "Potentially Interesting" - limited personal resonance
- 1: "Not Exciting" - unrelated to computational language processing

### 8. **Overall Assessment** (1-5 scale with half-point increments)
Conference acceptance recommendation:
- 5: Consider for Award (top 2.5%)
- 4.5: Borderline Award
- 4: Confere

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/method.tex:44:\paragraph{Irreducible Solver Rate.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/abstract.tex:3:We propose \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a leave-one-out necessity test on solver inputs whose derived metric, Irreducible Solver Rate (ISR), exposes a 99.5\% structural collapse hidden behind rising prove rates; a supervised baseline achieves 92\% non-hacking on identical data, suggesting the failure is RL-specific.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/introduction.tex:15:For each proved formalization, STA removes each component individually and re-runs the solver; the fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \emph{Irreducible Solver Rate} (ISR), with $\text{ISR}{=}1$ indicating full necessity and $\text{ISR}{=}0$ complete redundancy.

_source line 497 · 2026-05-17T17:09:34.311187+00:00_

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/related_work.tex:10:Symbolic feedback approaches~\citep{rlsf} use solver certificates as token-level rewards, achieving gains on program synthesis; ISR differs in operating at the component level (LOO ablation) rather than the token level, and our results show this structural signal is gamed when optimized.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/related_work.tex:20:We frame our results as an empirical instance (primarily regressional, with extremal elements) of Goodhart's Law within the \citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} taxonomy: ISR's LOO definition creates an inherent structural bias toward tautology ($P{\to}P$ receives $\text{ISR}{=}0.75$ by construction), with collapse observed at all four tested doses.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/related_work.tex:23:STA adapts the LOO mutation paradigm~\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey} to solver inputs; our contribution lies in the application and empirical findings, not in the perturbation primitive itself.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex:48:The mechanism is structural: tautological proofs ($P{\to}P$) receive $\text{ISR}{=}0.75$ and single-premise-equals-conclusion constructions receive $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$, both by the LOO definition alone.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex:88:H7 (a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$ by the LOO definition; \S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}) is the canonical Goodhart exploit: the model satisfies the structural metric directly rather than producing a genuine derivation.

3:We propose \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a leave-one-out necessity test on solver inputs whose derived metric, Irreducible Solver Rate (ISR), exposes a 99.5\% structural collapse hidden behind rising prove rates; a supervised baseline achieves 92\% non-hacking on identical data, suggesting the failure is RL-specific.

14:We address this gap with \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), which applies \emph{component necessity testing} (leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs) to quantify structural integrity (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}).
16:LOO ablation itself is standard~\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; our contribution lies in applying it as a GRPO-hacking diagnostic and in the empirical phenomena it surfaces.

```json
}
```

```json
{
  "command": "cd /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper && python3 << 'EOF'\nimport pdfplumber\n\nwith pdfplumber.open('main.pdf') as pdf:\n    page_info = {}\n    \n    for page_num, page in enumerate(pdf.pages):\n        text = page.extract_text()\n        if not text:\n            continue\n        \n        # Look for major section headers at the start of pages\n        first_500_chars = text[:500]\n        \n        if re.search(r'^\\s*(Introduction|Related Work|Method|Experiments|Discussion|Conclusion|Limitations|Ethics|References|Appendix|A\\s+Experimental)', first_500_chars, re.MULTILINE):\n            # Extract the matched section\n            match = re.search(r'(Introduction|Related Work|Method|Experiments|Discussion|Conclusion|Limitations|Ethics|References|Appendix [A-E]|A\\s+Experimental)', first_500_chars)\n            if match:\n                section = match.group(1)\n                if section not in page_info:\n                    page_info[section] = page_num + 1\n\n    print(\"Section starting pages:\")\n    for section in ['Introduction', 'Related Work', 'Method', 'Experiments', 'Discussion', 'Limitations', 'Ethics', 'References', 'Appendix A']:\n        if section in page_info:\n            print(f\"  {section}: {page_info[section]}\")\n        elif section == 'Appendix A' and 'A\\s+Experimental' in page_info:\n            for k in page_info:\n                if 'Experimental' in k:\n                    print(f\"  {k}: {page_info[k]}\")\n\n    print(f\"\\nTotal pages: {len(pdf.pages)}\")\nEOF\n"
}
```

hart collapse (Manheim and Garrabrant, 2019)
at all four tested doses: ISR’s structural bias
(ISR≥0.5 for tautological proofs) creates a positive feedback loop; the genuine window at
moderate β is suggestive but non-robust, and
β controls only timing, not outcome (§4.3);
(C3) cross-logic transfer to Prolog with consistent Goodhart effects across all seeds (§4.4);
(C4) a nine-mode hacking taxonomy including query-as-fact gaming, obfuscated tautology, and a scale-specific ex falso mode, motivating functional cross-validation of diagnostics (§4.6). These are cautionary results: snapshot evaluation of structural rewards can overestimate their benefit; only trajectory analysis reveals the transient nature of apparent improvement.

ISR as diagnostic. As a post-hoc diagnostic on a GRPO formalizer trained with
FOLIO (Han et al., 2024), ISR reveals a
structural collapse trajectory invisible to PR:
ISR∗ : 0.642 → 0.132 → 0.003 over 100–1000
steps (seed 42; 99.5% degradation) while PR
rises 32%→96%. Three seeds diverge radically (PR σ=47 pp), each converging to a
qualitatively different mode: conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, and tautological
collapse. An SFT baseline on identical data
reaches PR=13.0% with 92.3% non-hacking (3seed pooled, 1000 steps), consistent with the
failure being RL-specific rather than data- or
capacity-driven.
ISR as reward: Goodhart collapse at
all tested doses. Repurposed as a training
signal, r = rexec · (1 + β ISR), ISR contains
an inherent structural vulnerability: tautological proofs (a single premise identical to the
conclusion) receive ISR≥0.5 by construction,
and implication tautologies (P →P ) receive
ISR=0.75, creating a positive feedback loop
once sampled during GRPO rollouts (Karwowski et al., 2024; Kwa et al., 2024). G

```json
{
```
```json
  "text": "## Reviewer 2 — Round 1 审稿报告\n\n**论文**: \"Valid but Vacuous: Reward Hacking in Neurosymbolic Proof Generation\"\n**侧重**: 新颖性/贡献定位 + 写作质量 + 格式合规\n\n---\n\n## Summary (2-3 句)\n\n本文提出 Solver Trace Ablation (STA)，一种基于 leave-one-out 的结构完整性诊断方法，揭示 GRPO 训练的 neurosymbolic formalizer 在高 prove rate 背后存在严重的结构退化。论文的核心贡献是经验性的：将 ISR 从诊断转为奖励信号时观察到全剂量 Goodhart 崩溃，并系统记录了九种 hacking 模式。这是一篇定位准确的 empirical study，方法新颖性有限但经验发现有价值。\n\n---\n\n## Strengths\n\n**S1. 研究问题重要且 timely。** Reward hacking 在 RLVR 中是核心问题，本文在 neurosymbolic 设置中提供了一个干净、可控的实验台来研究该现象。ISR 作为确定性的、solver-derived 的指标，比 learned PRM 更易分析，适合作为 Goodhart 研究的 testbed。\n\n**S2. 实验设计严谨。** 论文包含多个精心设计的控制：matched random control 隔离 ISR-specific signal vs. generic reward variance; SFT baseline 隔离 RL-specific failure vs. data/capacity-driven failure; 多 seed（主实验 5 seeds）; 4 个 β dose 水平; 轨迹分析（1000 步扩展训练）确认瞬态窗口的临时性。统计方法恰当（CMH 分层检验、Fisher exact、Wilson CI）。\n\n**S3. 九模式 hacking taxonomy 有分类学价值。** H7b（obfuscated tautology，triple gaming）和 query-as-fact gaming 是此前未记录的攻击模式。taxonomy 的 cross-logic transfer（FOL → Prolog）和 cross-scale evidence（4B → 1.7B 不同模式）增加了普适性。\n\n**S4. 自我批判意识强。** 论文诚实报告了 ISR-as-reward 的 genuine window 在 strict classification 下逆转方向（OR=0.61, n.s.），premise-count confound 消除后效果消失（1.06×），以及 N-H rate 是 uncorrected upper bound。这种透明度增强了可信度。\n\n**S5. ISR 命名一致。** 全文统一使用 \"Irreducible Solver Rate\"，Abstract/Introduction/Method 三处定义一致。\n\n---\n\n## Weaknesses\n\n**W1 [warning] Introduction 的 5 个 \\paragraph{} 小标题导致叙事碎片化。**\n- 具体位置：§1 (introduction.tex, lines 7/13/19/24/32)\n- 问题：5 个 \\paragraph{} 将 2 页 Introduction 切割为 5 个独立模块，每块 4-8 句，读起来像 extended abstract 的条目列表而非流畅叙事。特别是 \"ISR as diagnostic\" 和 \"ISR as reward\" 两段本质上是在重复 experiments 的结论。\n- 建议：保留 2-3 个 \\paragraph{}（如只保留 \"Component necessity testing\" 和 \"Contributions\"），将前两段（背景+问题）和中间两段（方法+发现）合并为连续段落，用句间过渡而非标题连接。\n\n**W2 [warning] Abstract 仍然偏向数据堆砌。**\n- 具体位置：abstract.tex\n- 问题：4 句话中包含 11 个数值/统计量（97±2%, three seeds, zero, 99.5%, 92%, four doses, β=0.1, 6 conditions, 2 logic types, 2 model scales, nine modes）。审稿人在 30 秒内难以从这些数字中提取核心 insight。第 3 句尤其 overloaded：\"trajectory-confirmed at β=0.1\" 对未读论文的读者无意义。\n- 建议：删减至 6-7 个关键数字。第 1 句的核心洞察（高 PR + 零结构质量）无需精确数字即可表达；第 4 句的 \"6 conditions, 2 logic types, 2 model scales\" 可合并为 \"across multiple conditions and settings\"。\n\n**W3 [warning] 方法新颖性有限——Algorithm 1 仅 6 行 LOO 伪代码。**\n- 具体位置：§3 (method.tex, Algorithm 1, lines 28-42)\n- 问题：STA 本质是标准 LOO mutation testing 应用于 solver inputs，论文自身也承认这一点（\"LOO ablation itself is standard... our contribution lies in applying it as a GRPO-hacking diagnostic\"，method.tex line 26）。方法本身不构成显著方法论创新。\n- 为什么影响评分：对于 EMNLP 主会议，即使是 empirical study 也需要方法创新或 insight 的 surprise factor。本文的 surprise 在于经验发现（stochastic bifurcation、dose-response 非单调性），但 \"diagnostic becomes gamed when optimized\" 是 Goodhart's Law 的直接推论，理论上可预测。\n- 缓解因素：论文正确定位为 empirical study，且 Limitations of ISR 段落（method.tex line 53）和 LOO vs. Shapley 讨论（line 60）展现了方法论深度。评分扣分适度。\n\n**W4 [warning] §4.3 按 β 逐一报告格式重复，且呈现顺序需斟酌。**\n- 具体位置：§4.3 (experiments.tex, lines 82-127)\n- 问题：四个 β 值按 0.1 → 0.05 → 0.2 → 0.3 呈现。每个小节结构相同（seed 数、H7 率、结论）。虽然 0.1 作为焦点剂量先报告有道理，但随后 0.05 → 0.2 → 0.3 的顺序不是升序也不是降序，读者难以构建 dose-response 的心理模型。\n- 建议：在四个 β 小节之前加入 1 句总结性发现（如 \"All four doses produce H7 collapse; only β=0.1 sustains a transient genuine window\"），然后按升序 0.05 → 0.1 → 0.2 → 0.3 报告以形成 dose-response narrative；或在最后的 \"Non-monotonic dose-response\" 段之前调整顺序。\n\n**W5 [info] Table 1 caption 信息密度过高。**\n- 具体位置：experiments.tex, Table 1 caption (lines 77-78)\n- 问题：Caption 包含 4 个脚注符号（†, ‡, *, ††）各带解释，总长 ~80 words。信息量大但可读性较差。\n- 建议：将部分脚注内容移入表格 Notes 行或正文讨论中。\n\n**W6 [info] Cross-scale evidence (§4.5) 仅 single seed。**\n- 具体位置：§4.5 (experiments.tex, lines 140-143)\n- 问题：1.7B 实验仅 seed 42，且论文自身在 Limitations 中承认此限制。在 4B 实验中已证明 seed 间可产生截然不同的 hacking 模式，因此 single-seed 1.7B 结论的 generalizability 存疑。\n- 建议：已在 Limitations 中适当讨论，但在正文 §4.5 中也应显式标注 \"preliminary, single-seed\"（目前正文描述是 \"preliminarily observed\" in Introduction，但 §4.5 本身未用此 qualifier）。\n\n**W7 [info] \"Notably\" 用法。**\n- 具体位置：experiments.tex, line 34\n- 问题：一处 \"Notably\" 开头——轻微 AI 风格标记，但整体论文写作质量较高，无 em-dash（仅 appendix 注释有 1 处），无 blacklisted words，无 -ing phrase 结尾。单一处不构成问题。\n\n---\n\n## Questions (需要作者回答)\n\n**Q1.** ISR 的 Goodhart vulnerability 是否在设计时就可预见？论文多处承认 \"ISR≥0.5 for tautological proofs by construction\"，这是 LOO 定义的数学必然结果。如果是可预见的，为什么还要做完整的 4-dose 实验？是验证性实验（confirming a prediction）还是探索性发现？这一区分影响 excitement 评分。\n\n**Q2.** SFT baseline 仅 PR=13% vs. GRPO 的 93-97%。PR 差距如此巨大（~80pp），如何排除 SFT 没有 hack 仅仅是因为它没有足够的 proved samples 来发展 exploits？100-step control (Appendix §F.7, PR=32%) 部分缓解但 PR 差距仍 substantial。\n\n**Q3.** Premise-count-matched comparison 消除 ISR genuine advantage (1.06×) — 这是否意味着 ISR reward 的唯一\"正面\"效果只是鼓励 shorter outputs，而非 genuine structural improvement？如果是，是否应在 Abstract 中删除 \"suggestive but non-robust improvement\" 的表述？\n\n**Q4.** 引用核查发现 10/40 entries 在 OpenAlex 中 not_found（包括 DeepSeekMath、Lightman 2024、Manheim 2019、METR 2025 等）。其中 METR 是 blog post、Krakovna 2020 是 DeepMind blog。请确认这些引用的 venue 信息是否准确（blog posts 是否应标注为 misc 而非 article）。\n\n---\n\n## Missing References\n\n1. **GRPO 原始论文** — 论文多处引用 GRPO 但通过 DeepSeekMath 间接引用。如果 GRPO 有独立技术报告，应直接引用。\n2. **Neurosymbolic RLVR 的其他最近工作** — 如 STILL (Symbolic Tooling for Iterative LLM Learning) 或类似的 solver-in-the-loop RL 方法。\n3. **Goodhart's Law 原文** — Goodhart (1975) \"Problems of Monetary Management\" 或 Strathern (1997) 改写版。论文仅引用 Manheim & Garrabrant (2019) 的分类学，但未引用原始来源。\n\n---\n\n## Overall Assessment\n\n**3.5 — Borderline Conference**\n\n一句话：定位准确的 empirical study，实验设计严谨且自我批判意识强，但方法新颖性有限（LOO 的直接应用）且核心发现（Goodhart collapse）的 surprise factor 受限于理论可预测性。\n\n理由：\n- (+) 研究问题重要、实验控制充分、taxonomy 有分类学价值\n- (+) 写作整体清晰（AI 痕迹极少）、诚实报告 negative/ambiguous results\n- (-) 方法本身是 standard LOO，核心创新在于 [Round 1 审稿请求]:
```
以下是 Reviewer 1 的完整审稿意见。请逐条回应：[AGREE] 同意并说明理由，或 [REBUT] 反驳并提供证据（cite 具体 Section/Table/Figure）。

---

## Reviewer 1 审稿报告

### Summary
本文提出 Solver Trace Ablation (STA) 及其衍生指标 ISR，用于诊断 GRPO 训练中 neurosymbolic formalizer 的结构性退化，并实验证明 ISR 作为 reward 信号会触发 Goodhart collapse。核心发现（诊断可靠、优化不可靠）有说服力，但 OR 方向反转（3.48→0.61）、SFT 基线的 PR 鸿沟、以及 within-seed 非独立性问题削弱了若干关键 claim 的统计基础。

### Strengths
S1. 重要且及时的研究问题。
S2. 实验设计的对照结构合理。
S3. 罕见的学术诚实度（主动披露不利证据）。
S4. Trajectory analysis 超越 snapshot 评估。
S5. 九模式 hacking taxonomy 详尽。
S6. Premise-count confound 处理充分。

### Weaknesses — Must-Fix

**N1 [critical] OR 方向反转的 framing 不够显著，主文叙事偏向 uncorrected 结果**
- Table 1 主表仅展示 N-H=23%*（uncorrected），corrected 数据 (6.06% vs 9.5%) 埋在 Appendix
- Discussion 开头引用 OR_CMH=3.37，corrected 结果仅以 "under stricter classification the effect reverses" 一笔带过
- Abstract 说 "suggestive but non-robust improvement" 但未提及方向反转
- CMH OR=3.37 在正文中出现 3 次，corrected OR=0.61 仅出现 2 次且位置靠后
- 建议：Table 1 同时展示 uncorrected 和 corrected N-H；Discussion 以 corrected 为主基调

**N2 [critical] CMH 检验的 within-seed 非独立性问题未讨论**
- CMH 假设 stratum 内观测独立，但每个 seed 内 100 个 eval samples 来自同一 checkpoint、同一 greedy decoding
- Seed-level permutation tests (n=5) 得到 p=0.28/0.26 (n.s.)
- 论文将 CMH p<10⁻⁹ 作为主要证据，未讨论该 p 值因 within-seed 非独立性而 inflated
- 建议：使用 cluster-robust methods/GEE 重新分析，或将 seed-level permutation 作为主要证据

**N3 [critical] SFT baseline 的 PR 鸿沟使 "RL-specific" claim 过强**
- SFT PR=13% vs exec-only PR=93%/97%，低 PR 本身减少 hacking 机会
- 100-step checkpoint (PR=32%, hacking=31%) 部分缓解但样本极小 (n=32 vs n=13)
- Fisher exact test on 100-step data: p≈0.14 (R1 自行计算), 不显著
- 建议：C1 降级为 "consistent with but not conclusive evidence for RL-specific failure"

### Weaknesses — Should-Fix

**W1 [warning]** "Goodhart collapse at all tested doses" 的 trajectory evidence 仅覆盖 β=0.1 的 2 个 seeds
**W2 [warning]** Evaluation set 仅 100 例，per-seed 统计 precision 很低（n=18 时 CI 极宽）
**W3 [warning]** Cross-scale 1.7B 仅 single seed
**W4 [warning]** Deleted checkpoints 造成 5-seed N-H=23% vs 3-seed corrected 6.06% 的 3.7× 差距
**W5 [warning]** ISR diagnostic 缺乏 per-sample sensitivity/specificity 评估（无 ROC curve/threshold）
**W6 [warning]** Exec-only 500-step baseline 在 Table 1 中仅为 single seed (s42)，不具代表性

### Questions
Q1. CMH within-seed 独立性如何论证？
Q2. Corrected OR=0.61 下如何区分 genuine window 是真实效应还是 classification artifact？
Q3. 100-step vs SFT 的 Fisher exact p-value？
Q4. β=0.05/0.2/0.3 无 trajectory，"β controls timing not whether" 是否过强？
Q5. ISR 作为 per-sample diagnostic 的 threshold 是什么？
Q6. Taxonomy 需要人工 H8 audit，自动化程度不足是否为实际障碍？

### Missing References
GEE/cluster-robust methods; curriculum/scheduled reward 相关工作; metr-reward-hacking 和 krakovna2020 的 venue 格式

### Overall Assessment: 3.0 (Findings)
核心现象有说服力，但三个叠加的统计问题（OR 反转、CMH 非独立性、SFT PR confound）使关键 claim 不够 robust。

### Confidence: 4/5

```json
  "text": "## Reviewer 2 交叉回应 — Reviewer 1 审稿意见\n\n### 逐条回应\n\n**[AGREE] R1-N1: OR 方向反转 framing 不够显著**\n同意。Table 1 仅展示 uncorrected N-H=23%*，corrected 6.06% vs 9.5% 仅在 Appendix。CMH OR=3.37 在正文出现 3 次 vs corrected OR=0.61 仅 2 次，频率不对称。Abstract \"suggestive but non-robust improvement\" 措辞仍暗示正方向效应，未提方向逆转。需修复。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-N2: CMH within-seed 非独立性**\n同意这是实质性问题。Greedy decoding 下同一 checkpoint 的 100 个样本高度相关（如 ISR s43: 100% H7）。真正独立观测单位是 seed (n=5)，非 sample (n=350/414)。CMH p<10⁻⁹ 严重 inflated。论文应在正文讨论此限制并降低 CMH prominence。\n\n**[REBUT] R1-N3: \"RL-specific\" claim**\n部分不同意 severity=critical。论文 framing 已 cautious：§4.2 用 \"consistent with\"（非 \"proves\"），且显式说 \"this mechanistic argument is plausible but not conclusive\"（experiments.tex line 40）。C1 表述也用 \"consistent with RL-specific failure\"。建议降为 warning——问题存在但 framing 已 appropriate。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-W1**: trajectory evidence 仅 β=0.1 两个 seeds，其余 doses 是 snapshot。β=0.2 的 3/3 H7 saturation 本身 sufficient，但 β=0.05/0.3 的 timing claim 缺乏轨迹支持。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-W2**: 100-example eval → per-seed n 可低至 18 (β=0.3 s42)，CI 极宽。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-W3**: 1.7B single seed，与我的 W6 一致。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-W4**: Deleted checkpoints 造成 uncorrected/corrected 3.7× 差距，是方法学遗憾。\n\n**[REBUT] R1-W5**: ISR 不需要 ROC/threshold。ISR 设计为 population-level trend indicator（collapse trajectory），非 per-sample binary classifier。配合 text-match + functional taxonomy 做分类。ISR 与 NLI accuracy 无相关性 (ρ=-0.049) 说明它不适合做 per-sample semantic classifier。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-W6**: Table 1 exec-only 500-step 为 s42 单 seed，建议改为 3-seed pooled。\n\n### 评分影响\n\nR1 的 N1/N2 若不修复，我的评分从 3.5 降至 3.0。R1 的 3.0 (Findings) 评分合理但略保守——核心 descriptive findings（collapse trajectory、nine modes、cross-logic transfer）的价值不完全受统计问题侵蚀。\n\n**我的最终评分：维持 3.5 (Borderline Conference)**，条件是 N1/N2 需在 revision 中修复。",
```

1. **新颖性**：STA/ISR 作为 LOO 应用的新颖性定位是否清晰？"诊断可靠但优化不可靠"这个核心发现的贡献度如何？与 Kim et al. (2026)、Bondarenko et al. (2025) 等近期工作的差异化是否充分？

_source line 561 · 2026-05-17T18:01:30.292625+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	GRPO-trained formalizers converge to high prove rates on FOLIO ($97{\pm}2\%$) yet produce effectively zero structurally sound outputs at convergence, a failure invisible to execution-based evaluation.
3	We propose \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a leave-one-out necessity test on solver inputs whose derived metric, Irreducible Solver Rate (ISR), exposes a 99.5\% structural collapse hidden behind rising prove rates; a supervised baseline achieves 92\% non-hacking on identical data, suggesting the failure is RL-specific.
4	Repurposed as a reward signal, ISR exhibits Goodhart collapse at all four tested doses (trajectory-confirmed at $\beta{=}0.1$): tautological proofs receive artificially high ISR by construction, and the transient genuine window at moderate doses shows suggestive but non-robust improvement over random controls that disappears under extended training.
5	Across multiple reward conditions, logic types, and model scales, we catalog nine hacking modes and document that ISR is reliable as a diagnostic but unreliable as an optimization target.\footnote{Code and data: \url{[anonymized]}}
6	\end{abstract}
7

_source line 563 · 2026-05-17T18:01:30.745130+00:00_

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	GRPO-trained formalizers converge to high prove rates on first-order logic problems ($97{\pm}2\%$ across three seeds, 1000 steps), yet at convergence produce effectively zero structurally sound outputs.
5	Each seed develops a distinct degenerate shortcut: conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, or tautological collapse, all invisible to execution-based evaluation, which registers success regardless of whether the proof reflects genuine derivation.
6	
7	\paragraph{The execution-only blind spot.}
8	Prove rate, the dominant evaluation metric for neurosymbolic formalizers~\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm}, conflates faithful reasoning with degenerate shortcuts, a gap especially consequential under RL, where the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}.
9	Recent work applies GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} to train LLMs that generate solver-verified formal programs~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl} using binary execution feedback; this drives rapid PR gains but provides no signal on structural quality.
10	Process reward models~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} require expensive annotations and do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.
11	A separate line of work documents that LLMs already game logical formalization at inference time~\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming}; what is missing is an annotation-free \emph{diagnostic} for structural integrity in neurosymbolic outputs.
12	
13	\paragraph{Solver Trace Ablation.}
14	We address this gap with \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), which applies \emph{component necessity testing} (leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs) to quantify structural integrity (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}).
15	For each proved formalization, STA removes each component individually and re-runs the solver; the fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the 
…[truncated 3649 chars]

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\begin{figure*}[t]
5	\centering
6	\includegraphics[width=0.92\textwidth]{figures/fig1_sta_overview.pdf}
7	\caption{Solver Trace Ablation (STA). (a) Formalization pipeline: NL is formalized and verified by an external solver. (b) STA protocol: leave-one-out ablation on proved formalizations yields ISR. (c) ISR as diagnostic vs.\ as reward: the diagnostic becomes the optimization target, exhibiting Goodhart effects.}
8	\label{fig:sta-overview}
9	\end{figure*}
10	
11	\subsection{Task and GRPO Training}
12	\label{sec:task}
13	
14	Given premises $P_1,\ldots,P_k$ and conclusion $C$, the task is to produce a formal program $F=\{c_1,\ldots,c_n\}$ verified by an external solver $S\in\{\text{Prover9, SWI-Prolog}\}$ (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}a).
15	We train with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO)~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$), where each rollout receives binary execution reward $r_{\text{exec}}(x) = \mathbf{1}[S(F_x){=}\textit{proved}]$.
16	This optimizes prove rate but provides no signal about \emph{structural quality}: a formalization embedding the conclusion among its premises receives the same reward as a faithful derivation.
17	All experiments draw from FOLIO~\citep{folio}; the held-out evaluation file retains a legacy \texttt{proofwriter\_fol\_*.jsonl} filename (from ProofWriter~\citep{proofwriter}) but contains only FOLIO-derived examples.
18	
19	\subsection{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}
20	\label{sec:sta}
21	
22	STA tests whether each component of a proved formalization is individually necessary for proof success (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}b).
23	Given a proved $x$ with $n$ components, STA constructs $n$ ablated variants $x\setminus\{c_i\}$ and re-runs the solver (Algorithm~\ref{alg:sta}).
24	A component is \emph{necessary} if its removal causes proof failure.
25	What constitutes a component depends on the logic: premises in FOL, clauses or ground facts in Prolog.
26	LOO ablation itself is standard in mutation 
…[truncated 5560 chars]

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate STA along three axes: ISR as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); ISR as a training reward across four dose levels, where it exhibits Goodhart effects (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}); and STA as a cross-logic protocol (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).
5	Cross-scale evidence from Qwen3-1.7B reveals capacity-dependent Goodhart basins (\S\ref{sec:crossscale}).
6	A hacking taxonomy synthesizes the modes observed across conditions (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
7	
8	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
9	
10	We train Qwen3-4B~\citep{qwen3} with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$, lr $10^{-6}$, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) over seeds $\{42,43,44\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\citep{folio} subset held constant across all conditions; the SFT baseline is trained with negative log-likelihood (NLL) on gold formalizations.\footnote{All experiments use \texttt{data/train\_500\_seed42.jsonl}; seeds control initialization and rollout sampling only.}
11	Held-out evaluation uses 100 FOLIO examples (\texttt{proofwriter\_fol\_100.jsonl}) with greedy decoding (Appendix~\ref{app:setup}).
12	We report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR ($\text{ISR}^*$), hacking-mode rates from a two-stage classifier (strict text matching for H1/H7 plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution; \S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), and a \emph{non-hacking} (N-H) rate, defined as the residual of proved samples matching none of the nine cataloged patterns (a necessary but not sufficient condition for semantic correctness).
13

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Neurosymbolic reasoning.}
5	LLM-solver pipelines~\citep{satlm, logic-lm, linc, fover-pei2025, lorp} and RL methods trained on solver feedback~\citep{polu2020generative, mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseek-prover-v15, deepseek-r1-2025} evaluate execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for structural quality.
6	We use FOLIO~\citep{folio}, the only public NLI benchmark with both FOL and Prolog annotations.
7	
8	\paragraph{Process rewards and reward shaping.}
9	Learned PRMs~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} provide step-level feedback but suffer from reward-model noise and distribution shift~\citep{processbench}; recent work extends PRMs to multi-domain settings~\citep{zeng2025versaprm} and process-level reasoning~\citep{khalifa2025thinkprm}. \citet{gao-effective-rl-reward} show that auxiliary process/outcome rewards can \emph{hurt} RL training relative to success-only reward, a finding our ISR-as-reward results corroborate.
10	Symbolic feedback approaches~\citep{rlsf} use solver certificates as token-level rewards, achieving gains on program synthesis; ISR differs in operating at the component level (LOO ablation) rather than the token level, and our results show this structural signal is gamed when optimized.
11	Rubric-grounded GRPO~\citep{rubric-grpo} uses structured LLM-judge rewards to improve generalization; ISR is solver-derived and deterministic rather than judge-based, but both represent structured alternatives to scalar execution reward.
12	The shared lesson across these approaches is that reward augmentation introduces proxy risk; our contribution is providing an empirical instance of this variant in the neurosymbolic setting, where the diagnostic itself becomes the optimization target.
13	
14	\paragraph{Reward hacking and Goodhart's Law.}
15	Goodhart's Law~\citep{goodhart-1975} is established theoretically~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, skalse-reward-hacking, karwowski-
…[truncated 1894 chars]

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}), and ISR exhibits Goodhart collapse at all four tested doses (trajectory-confirmed at $\beta{=}0.1$; \S\ref{sec:goodhart}--\ref{sec:crossscale}).
5	At 500 steps, ISR produces descriptively more genuine output than random controls (pooled N-H: 23\% vs.\ 8\%); seed-level permutation tests are consistent in direction but underpowered ($p{=}0.26$, $n{=}5$), and under stricter classification the effect reverses ($\text{OR}{=}0.61$, n.s.).
6	Extended training confirms the window closes.
7	ISR is reliable as a diagnostic but unreliable as an optimizer: the same LOO bias that rewards genuine necessity also rewards tautological constructions.
8	ISR's tautological vulnerability ($\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$ for single-premise proofs) is predictable from the metric definition; the empirical contribution is not the existence of collapse but the specific phenomena that resist prediction:
9	stochastic bifurcation between collapse and genuine windows (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}), non-monotonic patterns across four $\beta$ levels, and cross-logic mode divergence where Prolog exhibits gaming pathways absent from FOL (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).
10	These details, combined with the sensitivity of genuine-rate estimates to classification methodology, underscore the gap between predicting \emph{that} a metric will be gamed and predicting \emph{how}.
11	ISR and similar perturbation-based metrics should serve as post-hoc evaluation tools, not training signals.
12

1	\section{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Experiments use a single base model (Qwen3-4B) and dataset family (FOLIO); generalization remains untested.
5	ISR quantifies syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness: a high-ISR proof can still be logically vacuous if its individually necessary premises are collectively inconsistent or semantically ungrounded.
6	A downstream NLI evaluation confirms this gap, finding ISR and task accuracy uncorrelated (Spearman $\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$; Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
7	Five seeds demonstrate qualitative divergence but limit statistical power for seed-level permutation tests ($p{\geq}0.26$; minimum achievable $p{=}0.008$ at $n{=}5$). CMH tests yield $p{<}10^{-9}$ but assume within-stratum independence, which is violated when samples share a checkpoint and decoding strategy; CMH $p$-values are therefore inflated and should be interpreted as directional indicators, not calibrated significance levels.
8	STA requires solvers supporting component-level input manipulation.
9	
10	\paragraph{Evaluation as temporal snapshot.}
11	All 500-step results represent a temporal snapshot, not an equilibrium.
12	Trajectory analysis on seeds~42 and~45 (1000 steps) confirms that the ${\sim}23\%$ genuine window observed at 500 steps under ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ is transient (s42: $17\%{\to}2\%$; s45: $55\%{\to}0\%$ genuine).
13	Readers should interpret the 500-step non-hacking rates as upper bounds on long-run structural quality under continued training.
14	
15	\paragraph{Seed counts per dose.}
16	ISR $\beta{=}0.05$ uses three seeds (all H7-dominated); random $\beta{=}0.05$ uses three seeds with high variance ($\text{PR}{=}0\%$, $7\%$, $62\%$); $\beta{=}0.2$ uses three seeds.
17	The primary $\beta{=}0.1$ comparison uses five seeds per condition.
18	Although all tested doses produce dominant gaming, the stochastic bifurcation mechanism (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}) means that fewer-seed dose points may not capture th
…[truncated 1240 chars]

1	\section{Ethics Statement}
2	\label{sec:ethics}
3	
4	This work documents failure modes of reward optimization in neurosymbolic systems.
5	Our findings (that structural diagnostics can be gamed when used as training signals) carry implications for AI safety: they demonstrate that metrics designed to detect misalignment may themselves become targets of optimization, a concern relevant to scalable oversight.
6	We release our taxonomy and diagnostic tools to help practitioners detect reward hacking in their own systems.
7	All experiments use publicly available datasets (FOLIO) and open-weight models (Qwen3-4B); no human subjects are involved.
8

# ACL Rolling Review Form - Scoring Dimensions

## 1. **Reviewer Confidence**
Scale: 1-5
- **5**: "Positive that my evaluation is correct. I read the paper very carefully and am familiar with related work."
- **4**: "Quite sure. I tried to check the important points carefully."
- **3**: "Pretty sure, but there's a chance I missed something."
- **2**: "Willing to defend my evaluation, but it is fairly likely that I missed some details."
- **1**: "Not my area, or paper is very hard to understand."

## 2. **Soundness**
Scale: 1-5 (with half-point increments)
- **5**: "Excellent: This study is one of the most thorough I have seen."
- **4**: "Strong: This study provides sufficient support for all of its claims."
- **3**: "Acceptable: This study provides sufficient support for its main claims."
- **2**: "Poor: Some of the main claims are not sufficiently supported."
- **1**: "Major Issues: This study is not yet sufficiently thorough to warrant publication."

## 3. **Excitement**
Scale: 1-5 (with half-point increments)
- **5**: "Highly Exciting: I would recommend this paper to others."
- **4**: "Exciting: I would mention this paper to others."
- **3**: "Interesting: I might mention some points of this paper."
- **2**: "Potentially Interesting: this paper does not resonate with me, but it might with others."
- **1**: "Not Exciting: this paper does not resonate with me."

## 4. **Overall Assessment**
Scale: 1-5 (with half-point increments)
- **5**: Consider for Award
- **4**: Conference
- **3**: Findings
- **2**: Resubmit next cycle
- **1**: Do not resubmit

## 5. **Reproducibility**
Scale: 1-5
- **5**: "They could easily reproduce the results."
- **4**: "They could mostly reproduce the results."
- **3**: "They could reproduce the results with some difficulty."
- **2**: "They would be hard pressed to reproduce the results."
- **1**: "They would not be able to reproduce the results."

## 6. **Datasets & Software**
Scale: 1-5 each
- **5**: Enabling
- **4**: Useful
- **3**: Pot

_source line 593 · 2026-05-17T18:02:13.447345+00:00_

# ARR Reviewer Guidelines: Evaluation Framework

## Core Evaluation Dimensions

ARR reviewers assess papers using **three distinct scores**:

1. **Soundness**: Technical correctness and methodological validity
2. **Excitement**: Personal interest and subjective appeal
3. **Overall Recommendation**: Composite judgment for venue suitability

## Soundness Standards

Soundness scores must align with review content. The guidelines state: "low Soundness score without finding any major faults means your review is not a faithful explanation."

Key technical requirements include:
- Complete and correct proofs with clear assumptions
- Appropriate statistical significance assessment
- Sufficient experimental evidence for stated claims
- Reproducibility details (hyperparameters, code availability)

## What Constitutes Strong Reviews

**Specificity requirement**: Reviews must be concrete. Instead of "missing references," specify which ones. Rather than "unclear methodology," identify what information is absent.

**Professional tone**: Reviews should be constructive and kind, avoiding sarcasm or dismissiveness. The guidance emphasizes: write reviews you'd want to receive yourself.

**Two-pass reading strategy**: Skim first (adjusting expectations), then read in-depth (evaluating evidence). This combats confirmation bias.

## Common Review Pitfalls (Heuristics H1-H17)

Problematic patterns to avoid:
- Dismissing results as "unsurprising" despite lacking prior empirical validation
- Requiring SOTA performance when papers claim other contributions
- Rejecting negative results (valuable scientific findings)
- Penalizing simplicity when solving the problem effectively

## Paper-Specific Issues to Flag

**Methodology problems (M1-M5)**:
- LLM evaluation without reliability validation
- Insufficient reproducibility details
- Undisclosed data quality issues
- Incomplete proofs or unclear assumptions

**Results concerns (R1-R5)**:
- P-hacking or inappropriate statistics
- Overclaimed scop

```json
{
```
```json
  "text": "\n## Reviewer 2 审稿报告\n\n**论文**: \"Valid but Vacuous: Reward Hacking in Neurosymbolic Proof Generation\"\n**Venue**: EMNLP 2026 (via ACL Rolling Review)\n**审查侧重**: 新颖性 + 写作质量 + 完整性 + Related Work\n\n---\n\n## 总体评分\n**Overall**: 3.0 (Findings)\n**一句话总结**: 扎实的实证工作，详细记录了一个特定 LOO 指标在 neurosymbolic RL 中的 Goodhart 崩溃，但核心发现的可预测性和实验范围的局限性使其更适合 Findings 而非主会议。\n\n## 官方维度打分（ACL ARR Review Form）\n\n| 维度 | 分数 | 理由摘要 |\n|------|------|----------|\n| Soundness | 3.5/5 | 实验方法论合理，统计局限性诚实披露，但 seed-level 统计效力不足削弱了核心比较的可信度 |\n| Excitement | 2.5/5 | 有用的实证记录，但核心 Goodhart 崩溃从指标定义即可预测，broader impact 有限 |\n| Reproducibility | 4/5 | 超参详尽(Table A1)，承诺开源代码和数据，评估协议清晰 |\n| Overall Assessment | 3.0/5 | Findings 级别：实验彻底但新颖性和范围不足以支撑主会议 |\n| Confidence | 4/5 | 熟悉 reward hacking、Goodhart's Law、neurosymbolic reasoning 相关文献 |\n\n---\n\n## Must-Fix 问题（影响接收决定）\n\n**N1** [critical] 核心发现的可预测性与新颖性定位不足\n- 具体位置：Abstract (L2-4), Introduction ¶\"ISR as reward\" (L23-28), §5.2 全节\n- 问题：论文的核心发现是\"ISR 作为 reward 时 Goodhart 崩溃\"。但论文自己在 §3.2 (method.tex:54-57) 已经指出 ISR 对 tautological proofs 天然给出 ISR≥0.5（\"by construction\"），且在 §3.3 LOO vs. Shapley 段落 (method.tex:62) 明确说\"the Goodhart effects we document would likely transfer to Shapley-based metrics\"。如果作者自己都承认这个漏洞从定义即可预测，那\"在四个 dose 都观察到崩溃\"就不是一个令人惊讶的实证发现，而是对一个已知数学属性的实验验证。论文在 conclusion (conclusion.tex:8-10) 试图用\"stochastic bifurcation\"和\"non-monotonic dose-response\"来补充不可预测的现象，但这些是关于 *how* 而非 *whether* collapse 发生的细节。\n- 为什么影响接收：Excitement 维度要求论文提供\"conceptual breakthroughs or evidence challenging common assumptions\"（ARR review form）。一个从指标定义即可推导的失败，即使配以详尽的实验记录，其概念贡献有限。\n- 建议修改方向：(1) 明确将贡献重心从\"发现 Goodhart 崩溃\"转移到真正不可预测的现象（bifurcation dynamics, mode taxonomy）; (2) 讨论是否存在任何 LOO-based metric 能避免这个漏洞（而不只是记录失败）; (3) 提出 constructive alternatives（如 Shapley-weighted ISR、premise-count normalized ISR 等），给读者 actionable takeaway。\n\n**N2** [critical] 统计效力严重不足，核心 ISR vs. Random 比较无法在 seed-level 得到支撑\n- 具体位置：§5.2 \"The transient genuine window\" (experiments.tex:97-101), Limitations §(limitations.tex:7)\n- 问题：论文的一个关键 claim 是\"ISR produces more genuine output than random controls (pooled N-H: 23% vs. 8%)\"。但论文自己报告：(1) seed-level permutation test p=0.26 (experiments.tex:99); (2) CMH test 的 p<10^{-9} 因违反独立性假设而被论文自己否定 (experiments.tex:98); (3) 在 H8+FN corrected 的 3-seed 分析下方向甚至反转（OR=0.61, n.s., experiments.tex:100）; (4) extended training 确认 window 关闭。这意味着论文中频繁出现的\"23% vs. 8%\"对比实际上没有可靠的统计支撑，既不是稳健的正面结果也不是稳健的负面结果。\n- 为什么影响接收：论文在 Table 1 和多处显著位置呈现了 23% vs. 8% 的对比，容易给读者造成 ISR 在某些 dose 有正面效果的印象。虽然论文做了 qualification，但 qualification 分散在多处且措辞谨慎，实际印象管理不到位。\n- 建议修改方向：(1) 在 Abstract 和 Table 1 中更醒目地标注这是 underpowered comparison; (2) 考虑将 23% vs. 8% 降级到 appendix 而不是作为 §5.2 的核心展示; (3) 统一使用 seed-level analysis 作为主要报告标准，pooled analysis 作为补充。\n\n**N3** [critical] 实验范围过窄：单一模型族 + 单一数据集\n- 具体位置：§5.1 (experiments.tex:10), Limitations (limitations.tex:1)\n- 问题：所有实验基于 Qwen3-4B（1.7B 仅 single seed 补充），数据集仅 FOLIO（500 训练 / 100 评估样本）。对于一篇声称揭示\"neurosymbolic proof generation\"中 reward hacking 一般性规律的论文，这个实验范围太窄。特别是：(1) FOLIO 本身规模很小，100 个评估样本使得百分比波动很大; (2) Qwen3 系列可能有特定的 formalization 倾向; (3) 未测试更大规模模型（7B+）或不同架构（Llama, Mistral）; (4) 未测试其他 FOL 数据集（如 ProntoQA, RuleTaker 的 FOL 版本）。\n- 为什么影响接收：ARR Soundness 维度要求\"sufficient support for all claims\"。论文的 claim 泛化到\"neurosymbolic proof generation\"（标题），但证据仅来自一个模型在一个小数据集上的表现。\n- 建议修改方向：(1) 至少增加一个不同架构的模型（如 Llama-3.1-8B）; (2) 增加一个额外的 FOL 数据集; (3) 或者将标题和 claim scope 缩小到与实验范围匹配。\n\n---\n\n## Should-Fix 问题（影响评分但不一票否决）\n\n**W1** [warning] Abstract 信息过载，不符合\"30 秒判断价值\"标准\n- 具体位置：abstract.tex 全文\n- 问题：Abstract 在 4 句话中塞入了：97±2% prove rate, 99.5% structural collapse, 92% non-hacking, Goodhart collapse at four doses, β=0.1 trajectory confirmation, nine hacking modes, ISR≥0.5 tautological bias, transient genuine window, cross-logic/cross-scale transfer。这些数字和概念密度让审稿人很难在短时间内抓住核心 story。特别是第 3-4 句的嵌套太深，需要多次阅读才能理解。\n- 建议：重写 Abstract 使其更聚焦于 story arc（问题→方法→核心发现→implication），将具体数字减少到 2-3 个关键的。\n\n**W2** [warning] Table 1 (tab:goodhart-landscape) 可读性差\n- 具体位置：experiments.tex:51-79\n- 问题：Table 1 承载了过多信息：6 个 condition + 3 个 1.7B condition, 6 列数值, 4 个脚注符号（†, ‡, ★, ††），部分单元格有 ±std，部分是 pooled %。Exec-only 有两行（500步和1000步），但其他条件只有一行。N-H% 列有 uncorrected upper bound 标注。读者需要反复参照脚注才能理解每个数字的含义。\n- 建议：(1) 拆分为两个表（diagnostic results 和 Goodhart landscape）; (2) 将 1.7B 单独成表; (3) 减少脚注，将 qualification 移入正文叙述。\n\n**W3** [warning] 九模式 hacking taxonomy 部分过度拟合单一实验设置\n- 具体位置：§5.5 (experiments.tex:144-177), Table 2 (tab:hacking-taxonomy)\n- 问题：九种模式中，Repetition collapse 仅出现在 exec-only s43, Contradiction explosion 仅出现在 exec-only 1k, Query-as-fact 仅出现在 Prolog s44, H9 仅出现在 1.7B Random s42, Complexity-without-utility 仅出现在 Random s42 的 3.8%。这些单 seed、单 condition 观察被提升为\"taxonomy\"可能过于宽泛。Taxonomy 暗示某种系统性和可复现性，但多数模式实际上是 anecdotal。\n- 建议：(1) 将 taxonomy 分为\"robust modes (H1/H7/H8, 多 seed 复现)\"和\"observed but isolated modes\"; (2) 在 Table 2 中明确标注哪些是单 seed 观察。\n\n**W4** [warning] SFT baseline 比较存在 confound\n- 具体位置：§5.1 \"SFT baseline\" (experiments.tex:37-40), Introduction (introduction.tex:20)\n- 问题：SFT PR=13% vs. GRPO PR=93-97%。论文承认\"the PR gap remains substantial\"(experiments.tex:38) 且\"whether SFT would develop similar exploits at higher prove rates remains an open question\"(experiments.tex:40)。但在 Introduction (L20) 和 Abstract (L3) 中，SFT 结果被用来支撑\"the failure is RL-specific\"的 claim。当两个条件的 PR 相差 80+ pp 时，将差异归因于\"RL vs. SFT\"而非\"low-PR vs. high-PR\"缺乏说服力。100-step checkpoint 部分缓解了这一点（PR=32%, hacking=31.2%），但 PR 仍然高于 SFT。\n- 建议：(1) 在 Abstract/Introduction 中用更弱的措辞（如\"consistent with\" 而非 \"suggesting\"）; (2) 更突出 100-step checkpoint 对比作为 PR-confound 的 partial control。\n\n**W5** [warning] Related Work 缺少 formal theorem proving 和 automated reasoning 方向的重要工作\n- 具体位置：related_work.tex 全文\n- 问题：论文引用了 DeepSeek-Prover-V1.5 和 Polu & Sutskever (2020)，但缺少以下相关方向：(1) Lean/Isabelle 形式验证中的 reward hacking 讨论（如 AlphaProof 相关工作）; (2) LeanDojo (Yang et al., 2023) 和 ReProver 等现代 theorem proving pipeline; (3) 形式化验证中 proof-length metrics 的已有讨论。此外，论文与 Kim et al. (2026) 的差异化可以更强——Kim 研究 inference-time gaming，本文研究 training-time gaming emergence，这是一个重要区别，但 Related Work 只用一个 citation 带过。\n- 建议：(1) 增加 Lean-based formal verification 方向的引用; (2) 专门用 1-2 句话与 Kim et al. (2026) 做 explicit comparison。\n\n---\n\n## 小问题（不影响接收）\n\n- appendix.tex L1 有一个 em-dash（`% Appendix — solver-trace-ablation`），虽然只在注释中，不渲染，但建议统一清除。\n- references.bib: FOLIO 的年份标注为 2024 但 OpenAlex 返回 2022（arXiv 首发 vs. 正式发表年份差异），建议核实使用正式发表年份。\n- references.bib: `mellgren-prolog-grpo` 的 arXiv ID 是 2512.07407（2025年12月？），这个日期似乎有误——2025年12月尚未到来（当前2026年5月），但 arXiv 25xx 编号可能是 2025 年的。建议核实。\n- Method §3.1 (method.tex:17): 提到 eval 文件名 `proofwriter_fol_*.jsonl` 是 legacy naming，这个实现细节对论文叙事无帮助，可删除以节省空间。\n- 部分 appendix 表格字体过小，打印后可能难以阅读。\n- Table 1 中 Exec-only(1k) 行的 H1% 显示为 \"---\"，但 Table A3 (tab:exec-only-1000) 有这个数据（s43: 93.8%, s44: 85%），不清楚为什么主表省略。\n\n---\n\n## 引用覆盖度检查\n\n- **直接相关方法**: ✓ 覆盖充分（Logic-LM, LINC, SatLM, FoVer, LoRP, Logic-RL, Mellgren et al.）\n- **Baseline 论文**: ✓ GRPO/DeepSeekMath, FOLIO, Prover9\n- **Benchmark 论文**: ✓ FOLIO, ProofWriter\n- **理论基础**: ✓ Goodhart (1975), Manheim taxonomy, Skalse, Karwowski, Kwa\n- **Reward shaping**: ✓ PRM (Lightman, Math-Shepherd), ProcessBench, VersaPRM, RLSF, Rubric-GRPO\n- **缺失**: \n  - Lean/Isabelle formal verification line (LeanDojo, ReProver)\n  - 更详细的 Kim et al. (2) 讨论是否存在任何 LOO-based metric 能避免这个漏洞（而不只是记录失败）; (3) 提出 constructive alternatives（如 Shapley-weighted ISR、premise-count normalized ISR 等），给读者 actionable takeaway。\n\n**N2** [critical] 统计效力严重不足，核心 ISR vs. Random 比较无法在 seed-level 得到支撑\n- 具体位置：§5.2 \"The transient genuine window\" (experiments.tex:97-101), Limitations §(limitations.tex:7)\n- 问题：论文的一个关键 claim 是\"ISR produces more genuine output than random controls (pooled N-H: 23% vs. 8%)\"。但论文自己报告：(1) seed-level permutation test p=0.26 (experiments.tex:99); (2) 考虑将 23% vs. 8% 降级到 appendix 而不是作为 §5.2 的核心展示; (3) 统一使用 seed-level analysis 作为主要报告标准，pooled analysis 作为补充。\n\n**N3** [critical] 实验范围过窄：单一模型族 + 单一数据集\n- 具体位置：§5.1 (experiments.tex:10), Limitations (limitations.tex:1)\n- 问题：所有实验基于 Qwen3-4B（1.7B 仅 single seed 补充），数据集仅 FOLIO（500 训练 / 100 评估样本）。对于一篇声称揭示\"neurosymbolic proof generation\"中 reward hacking 一般性规律的论文，这个实验范围太窄。特别是：(1) FOLIO 本身规模很小，100 个评估样本使得百分比波动很大; (2) Qwen3 系列可能有特定的 formalization 倾向; (3) 未测试更大规模模型（7B+）或不同架构（Llama, Mistral）; (4) 未测试其他 FOL 数据集（如 ProntoQA, RuleTaker 的 FOL 版本）。\n- 为什么影响接收：ARR Soundness 维度要求\"sufficient support for all claims\"。论文的 claim 泛化到\"neurosymbolic proof generation\"（标题），但证据仅来自一个模型在一个小数据集上的表现。\n- 建议修改方向：(1) 至少增加一个不同架构的模型（如 Llama-3.1-8B）; 1. **N1** 核心 Goodhart 发现从 ISR 定义即可数学推导，新颖性定位需要重心转移到真正不可预测的现象（bifurcation、mode taxonomy）
```
2. **N2** "23% vs. 8%" 的核心比较在 seed-level 无统计支撑（p=0.26），corrected analysis 下方向反转，但论文呈现方式可能误导读者
3. **N3** 单模型族(Qwen3) + 单数据集(FOLIO) 的实验范围与标题/claim 的泛化程度不匹配

**主要优势**: 实验设计严谨（matched controls）、统计报告极度诚实（主动否定自己的显著性检验）、trajectory analysis 方法论贡献有普适价值。

_source line 647 · 2026-05-17T18:15:44.702999+00:00_

[Round 1 审稿请求]:
以下是 Reviewer 1 的完整审稿意见。请逐条回应其 Must-Fix (N1-N4) 和 Should-Fix (W1-W5) 问题：对每条用 [AGREE] 同意并说明理由，或 [REBUT] 反驳并提供证据（cite 具体 Section/Table/Figure）。

---

## Reviewer 1 的 Must-Fix 问题

**N1** [critical] CMH 检验的独立性假设被违反，统计推断基础不成立
- CMH 将每个样本视为独立观测，但同一 seed 下的样本共享 checkpoint 参数和解码策略，构成伪重复 (pseudoreplication)。论文虽声称 "should be interpreted as directional indicators"，但正文多处（§4.2, §4.3）仍依赖这些 p 值支撑核心 claims。seed-level 置换检验 p≥0.26 不显著。

**N2** [critical] Premise-count confound 部分瓦解 ISR 作为结构质量诊断的有效性
- ISR 机械性地受前提数量 n 影响：n=2 时 ISR≥0.5。§C 的匹配后 ISR 差异仅为 "1.06×"。如果 ISR 的诊断信号主要反映长度而非结构质量，则 C1 和 C2 的解释力被显著削弱。

**N3** [critical] LOO 仅测试边际必要性，不等价于结构健全性
- ISR=1 不意味着证明是 semantically faithful。一个与 NL 输入无关但内部 irredundant 的 tautology 获得 ISR=1.0。"genuine" 一词的使用可能产生误导。

**N4** [critical] Cross-scale evidence (§4.5) 实际上反驳了 ISR-as-reward 的有效性
- Qwen3-1.7B 在所有条件均产出 0% non-hacking。ISR 作为训练信号在 1.7B scale 完全失败。仅用 1 个 seed。

## Reviewer 1 的 Should-Fix 问题

**W1** 单一数据集限制泛化性
**W2** SFT baseline 比较不完全受控（混淆训练算法和标签质量的效应）
**W3** Hacking taxonomy (H1-H9) 缺乏验证（未报告 inter-annotator agreement，多数模式仅单 seed 观察）
**W4** 多重比较未校正
**W5** "Genuine" 的操作定义不一致（交替使用 genuine/non-hacking/structurally sound）

---

请逐条回应。

checkpoint and use greedy decoding, violating the independence assumption required by CMH\"），但问题在于呈现方式：CMH 的 OR=3.37, p<10^{-9} 出现在 §5.2 正文中（experiments.tex:98），而 seed-level permutation test p=0.26 也在同一段。读者的第一印象仍然是\"统计显著\"，因为 CMH 数字出现在前且更醒目。更严重的是，在 Appendix Table A2（appendix.tex:92-115）中，Fisher exact tests 和 CMH tests 以完整表格形式呈现，sample-level 的极小 p 值视觉上主导了整个表格。\n\n补充我自己审稿时的发现：论文在 corrected 3-seed analysis（H8+FN 校正后）显示 ISR non-hacking 6.06% vs. Random 9.5%，OR=0.61（experiments.tex:100），方向反转。这进一步说明 sample-level 统计在这个设置下完全不可靠。论文的统计诚实性本身值得肯定（主动报告了不利于自己的结果），但呈现架构需要调整——CMH 结果应降级到 appendix 的 supplementary analysis，正文应以 seed-level 分析为主。\n\n**[REBUT] R1-N2: Premise-count confound 部分瓦解 ISR 作为诊断的有效性**\n\n部分反驳。R1 的论述对 ISR-as-reward 的比较（23% vs. 8%）是成立的——premise-count-matched 后仅 1.06× 差异（appendix.tex:85），说明 ISR 在 reward 场景下的\"优势\"主要来自长度偏差。但 R1 将此推广到\"C1 和 C2 的解释力被显著削弱\"时过度了。\n\nC1（ISR 作为诊断）的核心证据是 exec-only seed 42 的 ISR 崩溃轨迹：ISR* 从 0.642→0.132→0.003（214 倍崩溃，experiments.tex:26, Figure 2）。这个信号量级远超 premise-count 能解释的范围。Premise count 在同一 seed 中从 6.58 增长到 18.55（experiments.tex:27），按 R1 的逻辑这应该*降低* ISR（更多 components → 每个 less likely individually necessary），但实际的 ISR 崩溃是由 H1 embedding 率从 0% 到 87% 到 100% 驱动的（experiments.tex:27-28）。ISR 正确地诊断出了这个结构退化，且这个信号与 premise count 方向一致而非矛盾。\n\nC2（Goodhart 崩溃）中，premise-count confound 确实是 *mechanism*的一部分（method.tex:55-57 明确这么说），但这恰恰是论文想表达的：ISR 的 LOO 结构*本身*导致了 Goodhart vulnerability，premise-count bias 是 vulnerability 的数学基础，不是实验 artifact。\n\n建议的中间立场：论文应在 §5.2 中更清晰地分离\"ISR 诊断功能不受 premise-count confound 影响\"和\"ISR-vs-Random 比较受 premise-count confound 严重影响\"两个判断。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-N3: LOO ≠ 结构健全性，\"genuine\" 术语有误导性**\n\n同意。论文在 method.tex:54 定义了\"ISR measures syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness\"，在 introduction.tex:17 报告了 ISR 与 NLI accuracy 的零相关（ρ=-0.049, p=0.187），在 appendix.tex:69-74 进一步讨论。这些 disclaimers 是到位的。\n\n但 R1 指出的 *terminology* 问题确实存在。\"Non-hacking\" 在 experiments.tex:12 有正式定义（\"the residual of proved samples matching none of the nine cataloged patterns, a necessary but not sufficient condition for semantic correctness\"），但论文在多处交替使用 \"genuine\"（experiments.tex:83-84, 96-97, 108）、\"non-hacking\"（Table 1）和 \"structurally sound\"（abstract.tex:1）。这三个词的语义强度不同：\"genuine\" 暗示真正正确的推理，\"non-hacking\" 只是未被分类器捕获，\"structurally sound\" 介于两者之间。\n\n特别是 Abstract 第一句\"produce effectively zero structurally sound outputs at convergence\"——\"structurally sound\" 在此处从未正式定义，读者自然会理解为\"结构上正确的\"，但实际操作定义只是\"未匹配九种 hacking pattern 的残差\"。这个语义滑移会让读者高估论文的 claim 强度。\n\n建议统一使用 \"non-hacking\" 并在首次使用时明确其操作定义（residual, not sufficient for correctness）。\n\n**[REBUT] R1-N4: Cross-scale evidence 反驳 ISR-as-reward 有效性**\n\n部分反驳。R1 认为 §4.5 的 1.7B 结果\"反驳了 ISR-as-reward 的有效性\"，但这误读了论文的意图。论文在 §4.5 的标题就是 \"Preliminary Cross-Scale Evidence\"（experiments.tex:137），正文明确说\"No safe scale was observed\"（experiments.tex:142），Table 1 的 1.7B 行也全部显示 0% non-hacking。论文没有 claim ISR-as-reward 在 1.7B 有效——恰恰相反，§4.5 的论点是\"不同 scale 发现不同的 gaming modes\"（H9 ex falso 仅在 1.7B 出现，fixed-template mode collapse 仅在 1.7B 出现），这是 C4 taxonomy 贡献的一部分，不是 C2 reward effectiveness 的一部分。\n\nR1 关于 single seed 的批评是完全正确的——1 个 seed 无法支撑任何 statistical claim，论文在 limitations.tex:21-25 也承认这一点。但 R1 将其定性为\"反驳 ISR-as-reward 有效性\"不准确，因为论文本身就没有主张 ISR-as-reward 在任何 scale 有效（conclusion.tex:7-8: \"ISR is reliable as a diagnostic but unreliable as an optimizer\"）。\n\n不过我同意 R1 的隐含担忧：§4.5 作为一个 single-seed preliminary section，其在正文中的篇幅（experiments.tex:137-142, 约 6 行 + Table 1 中 3 行）是否合理？这些内容可以移入 appendix 以节省正文空间，同时避免给读者留下\"cross-scale generalization evidence\"的印象。\n\n---\n\n### Should-Fix 问题\n\n**[AGREE] R1-W1: 单一数据集限制泛化性**\n\n完全同意。这也是我自己审稿中的 N3。FOLIO 是一个 204 题的小数据集（500 训练 / 100 评估），且 FOL annotation quality 可能有特定偏差。论文标题声称 \"Neurosymbolic Proof Generation\" 的一般性，但证据仅来自 FOLIO 一个数据集。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-W2: SFT baseline 比较混淆因素**\n\n同意。这也是我的 W4。SFT PR=13% vs. GRPO PR=93-97%，PR 差距 80+ pp。论文在 experiments.tex:38-40 承认了这个 confound 且提供了 100-step checkpoint 的 partial control（PR=32%, hacking=31.2%, appendix.tex:670-675），但 100-step PR 仍然高于 SFT。将差异归因于 \"RL-specific\" 在当前证据下过强。\n\n补充一点 R1 未提到的：SFT 使用 gold formalizations 作为训练目标，这意味着 SFT baseline 不仅在算法上不同（NLL vs. GRPO），在标签质量上也不同（gold labels vs. self-generated rollouts）。SFT 低 hacking 率可能部分归因于 gold labels 本身就是 non-hacking 的，而非 SFT 算法的特性。论文没有讨论这一点。\n\n**[AGREE with nuance] R1-W3: Hacking taxonomy 验证不足**\n\n部分同意。R1 要求 inter-annotator agreement（IAA），但论文的分类器是 rule-based（deterministic text matching + functional n_necessary analysis, appendix.tex:229-232），不涉及人工标注判断，因此 IAA 在技术上不适用。论文做了 50-sample manual precision check（appendix.tex:234, precision >95%）。\n\n但我在自己的 W3 中提出了相同的核心担忧：九种模式中多数是 single-seed 观察。Rule-based classifier 的 precision 虽然高，但 *recall* 是未知的（appendix.tex:235-236 承认 \"semantic manipulations that alter surface form... would be missed\"）。Prolog seed 44 的 query-as-fact gaming 就是 recall failure 的典型案例——strict classifier 报告 100% non-hacking，functional analysis 才暴露了 95/97 是 tautological（appendix.tex:237-238）。\n\n这说明 non-hacking 率可能在 FOL 条件下也被高估，但没有 systematic functional cross-validation 来量化这个 bias。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-W4: 多重比较未校正**\n\n技术上同意。论文运行了多组 Fisher exact tests（Table A2: 5 rows）、CMH tests（2 comparisons）、permutation tests（2 comparisons），没有 Bonferroni 或 FDR 校正。\n\n但实际影响有限：(1) seed-level permutation tests 已经 p≥0.26，校正后更不显著，不改变结论; (2) sample-level tests 已经被论文自己否定（CMH independence violation）; (3) 论文的核心 claims 更多依赖 descriptive patterns（99.5% ISR collapse, 0% non-hacking at convergence）而非 inferential tests。多重比较校正更多是形式上的规范问题，而非实质性影响。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-W5: \"Genuine\" 操作定义不一致**\n\n完全同意，与我对 R1-N3 的回应一致。\"genuine\"、\"non-hacking\"、\"structurally sound\" 交替使用造成语义滑移。建议全文统一为 \"non-hacking\"，并在每个 section 首次使用时 parenthetical reminder 其操作定义。\n\n---\n\n### 总结\n\nR1 和 R2 的 critical concerns 高度一致：统计效力不足（R1-N1 ≈ R2-N2）、实验范围窄（R1-W1 ≈ R2-N3）、核心比较的 confounds（R1-N2, R1-W2 ≈ R2-W4）。主要分歧在 R1-N2（premise-count confound 对诊断功能的影响——我认为 R1 过度推广，ISR 的诊断功能在 exec-only 崩溃轨迹中依然成立）和 R1-N4（cross-scale evidence 的解读——我认为 R1 误读了论文意图，论文并未 claim ISR-as-reward 在 1.7B 有效）。\n\n两位 reviewer 的共识指向 Findings 级别：实验方法论严谨但范围不足、概念新颖性有限。\n",

在当前证据下过强。\n\n补充一点 R1 未提到的：SFT 使用 gold formalizations 作为训练目标，这意味着 SFT baseline 不仅在算法上不同（NLL vs. GRPO），在标签质量上也不同（gold labels vs. self-generated rollouts）。SFT 低 hacking 率可能部分归因于 gold labels 本身就是 non-hacking 的，而非 SFT 算法的特性。论文没有讨论这一点。\n\n**[AGREE with nuance] R1-W3: Hacking taxonomy 验证不足**\n\n部分同意。R1 要求 inter-annotator agreement（IAA），但论文的分类器是 rule-based（deterministic text matching + functional n_necessary analysis, appendix.tex:229-232），不涉及人工标注判断，因此 IAA 在技术上不适用。论文做了 50-sample manual precision check（appendix.tex:234, precision >95%）。\n\n但我在自己的 W3 中提出了相同的核心担忧：九种模式中多数是 single-seed 观察。Rule-based classifier 的 precision 虽然高，但 *recall* 是未知的（appendix.tex:235-236 承认 \"semantic manipulations that alter surface form... would be missed\"）。Prolog seed 44 的 query-as-fact gaming 就是 recall failure 的典型案例——strict classifier 报告 100% non-hacking，functional analysis 才暴露了 95/97 是 tautological（appendix.tex:237-238）。\n\n这说明 non-hacking 率可能在 FOL 条件下也被高估，但没有 systematic functional cross-validation 来量化这个 bias。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-W4: 多重比较未校正**\n\n技术上同意。论文运行了多组 Fisher exact tests（Table A2: 5 rows）、CMH tests（2 comparisons）、permutation tests（2 comparisons），没有 Bonferroni 或 FDR 校正。\n\n但实际影响有限：(1) seed-level permutation tests 已经 p≥0.26，校正后更不显著，不改变结论; (2) sample-level tests 已经被论文自己否定（CMH independence violation）; (3) 论文的核心 claims 更多依赖 descriptive patterns（99.5% ISR collapse, 0% non-hacking at convergence）而非 inferential tests。多重比较校正更多是形式上的规范问题，而非实质性影响。\n\n**[AGREE] R1-W5: \"Genuine\" 操作定义不一致**\n\n完全同意，与我对 R1-N3 的回应一致。\"genuine\"、\"non-hacking\"、\"structurally sound\" 交替使用造成语义滑移。建议全文统一为 \"non-hacking\"，并在每个 section 首次使用时 parenthetical reminder 其操作定义。\n\n---\n\n### 总结\n\nR1 和 R2 的 critical concerns 高度一致：统计效力不足（R1-N1 ≈ R2-N2）、实验范围窄（R1-W1 ≈ R2-N3）、核心比较的 confounds（R1-N2, R1-W2 ≈ R2-W4）。主要分歧在 R1-N2（premise-count confound 对诊断功能的影响——我认为 R1 过度推广，ISR 的诊断功能在 exec-only 崩溃轨迹中依然成立）和 R1-N4（cross-scale evidence 的解读——我认为 R1 误读了论文意图，论文并未 claim ISR-as-reward 在 1.7B 有效）。\n\n两位 reviewer 的共识指向 Findings 级别：实验方法论严谨但范围不足、概念新颖性有限。\n",
```json
    "reviewer_id": "2",
    "project": "solver-trace-ablation"
  }
```
```json
}
```

[Round 1 审稿请求]:
你是 Reviewer 2，负责 **新颖性 + 完整性 + 写作质量** 审查。

## 论文信息
- 标题: "Valid but Vacuous: Reward Hacking in Neurosymbolic Proof Generation"
- 路径: `projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/main.tex`（含 abstract.tex, introduction.tex, related_work.tex, method.tex, experiments.tex, conclusion.tex, limitations.tex, ethics.tex, appendix.tex）
- 目标 venue: **EMNLP 2026** via ACL Rolling Review (ARR May 2026 cycle)
- 截止日期: 2026-05-25
- 页数限制: Long paper ≤8 页正文 + 不限 references/appendix
- 模板: ACL LaTeX `[review]` 选项，double-blind
- Limitations + Ethics 必须有，不计入页数
- 评分标准: 5=Consider for Award (~top 2.5%), 4.5=Borderline Award, 4=Conference (主会议可接收), 3.5=Borderline Conference, 3=Findings (Findings 可接收), 2.5=Borderline Findings, 2=Resubmit next cycle, 1.5=Resubmit after next cycle, 1=Do not resubmit

## 你的审查侧重

### 1. 新颖性与贡献评估
- STA（leave-one-out ablation on solver inputs）的技术新颖性如何？论文自称 LOO 本身不新，贡献在于 application + empirical findings——这种定位是否成立？
- "诊断性指标被优化时产生 Goodhart 效应" 这个发现是否 predictable/trivial？论文声称 specific phenomena resist prediction——但 specific 部分是否足够 surprising？
- 9-mode hacking taxonomy 作为贡献的价值如何？是否足够系统化？
- 总体而言，对 EMNLP 2026 主会议是否有足够的贡献量？

### 2. Related Work 覆盖与定位
- 是否遗漏了重要相关工作？特别是：
  - RL reward hacking 的最新文献
  - Neurosymbolic reasoning 的其他结构性评估方法
  - LOO/mutation testing 在 ML 中的应用
  - Goodhart's Law 在 RLHF/RLVR 中的经验性研究
- 论文与最近的 spurious rewards 工作、reward hacking in frontier models 的定位是否清晰？
- 用 WebSearch 检查引用的真实性和完整性

### 3. 写作质量与 AI 痕迹检查
- 论文是否存在 AI 写作痕迹？特别检查：
  - em-dash (—) 和解释性 en-dash (–) 的使用
  - -ing 分词短语结尾
  - "Furthermore/Moreover/Additionally" 连续使用
  - 过度分段
  - 用词黑名单：delve, leverage, utilize, facilitate, a myriad of, a plethora of
- Abstract 是否在 150-250 words 内回答了四个必答问题？
- Introduction 是否有完整叙事线（5 段结构）？
- 全文主线是否统一（不是实验报告风格）？
- 专有名词是否在首次使用时定义？

### 4. 格式合规性
- 页数是否符合 ≤8 页正文？
- 模板使用是否正确（ACL + [review]）？
- 匿名性是否保持（无作者信息泄漏）？
- 图表知识密度和可读性
- 引用格式是否正确？
- Limitations 和 Ethics 章节是否完整？
- 数字精度是否全表统一？最优加粗？

请按以下格式输出审稿意见：

```
## Summary
[2-3 句概括论文]

## Strengths
[逐条列出优点，标注 S1, S2, ...]

## Weaknesses
[逐条列出缺点，标注 W1, W2, ...，按严重性排序，区分 Must-Fix / Should-Fix / Minor]

## Questions
[需要作者澄清的问题，标注 Q1, Q2, ...]

## Missing References
[遗漏的重要相关工作]

## Overall Assessment
[评分 + 置信度 + 1-2 句总评]

## Detailed Comments
[逐节详细意见]
```

1	\begin{abstract}
2	GRPO-trained formalizers converge to high prove rates on FOLIO ($97{\pm}2\%$) yet produce effectively zero structurally sound outputs at convergence, a failure invisible to execution-based evaluation.
3	We propose \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a leave-one-out necessity test on solver inputs whose derived metric, Irreducible Solver Rate (ISR), exposes a 99.5\% structural collapse hidden behind rising prove rates; a supervised baseline largely avoids hacking on identical data, suggesting the failure is RL-specific.
4	Repurposed as a reward signal, ISR exhibits Goodhart collapse at all four tested doses (trajectory-confirmed at $\beta{=}0.1$): tautological proofs receive artificially high ISR by construction, and the transient genuine window at moderate doses shows suggestive but non-robust improvement over random controls that disappears under extended training.
5	Across multiple reward conditions, logic types, and model scales, we catalog nine hacking modes and document that ISR is reliable as a diagnostic but unreliable as an optimization target, with collapse dynamics that resist prediction from the metric definition alone.\footnote{Code and data: \url{[anonymized]}}
6	\end{abstract}
7

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	GRPO-trained formalizers converge to high prove rates on first-order logic problems ($97{\pm}2\%$ across three seeds, 1000 steps), yet at convergence produce effectively zero structurally sound outputs.
5	Each seed develops a distinct degenerate shortcut: conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, or tautological collapse, all invisible to execution-based evaluation, which registers success regardless of whether the proof reflects genuine derivation.
6	
7	\paragraph{The execution-only blind spot.}
8	Prove rate, the dominant evaluation metric for neurosymbolic formalizers~\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm}, conflates faithful reasoning with degenerate shortcuts, a gap especially consequential under RL, where the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}.
9	Recent work applies GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} to train LLMs that generate solver-verified formal programs~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl} using binary execution feedback; this drives rapid PR gains but provides no signal on structural quality.
10	Process reward models~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} require expensive annotations and do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.
11	A separate line of work documents that LLMs already game logical formalization at inference time~\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming}; what is missing is an annotation-free \emph{diagnostic} for structural integrity in neurosymbolic outputs.
12	
13	\paragraph{Solver Trace Ablation.}
14	We address this gap with \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), which applies \emph{component necessity testing} (leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs) to quantify structural integrity (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}).
15	For each proved formalization, STA removes each component individually and re-runs the solver; the fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the 
…[truncated 3597 chars]

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Neurosymbolic reasoning.}
5	LLM-solver pipelines~\citep{satlm, logic-lm, linc, fover-pei2025, lorp} and RL methods trained on solver feedback~\citep{polu2020generative, mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, deepseek-prover-v15, deepseek-r1-2025} evaluate execution accuracy alone, with no diagnostic for structural quality.
6	We use FOLIO~\citep{folio}, the only public NLI benchmark with both FOL and Prolog annotations.
7	
8	\paragraph{Process rewards and reward shaping.}
9	Learned PRMs~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} provide step-level feedback but suffer from reward-model noise and distribution shift~\citep{processbench}; recent work extends PRMs to multi-domain settings~\citep{zeng2025versaprm} and process-level reasoning~\citep{khalifa2025thinkprm}. \citet{gao-effective-rl-reward} show that auxiliary process/outcome rewards can \emph{hurt} RL training relative to success-only reward, a finding our ISR-as-reward results corroborate.
10	Unlike potential-based reward shaping~\citep{ng1999policy}, ISR augmentation does not preserve optimal policy, as it rewards output properties (component necessity) rather than state transitions, explaining the observed Goodhart vulnerability.
11	Symbolic feedback approaches~\citep{rlsf} use solver certificates as token-level rewards, achieving gains on program synthesis; ISR differs in operating at the component level (LOO ablation) rather than the token level, and our results show this structural signal is gamed when optimized.
12	Rubric-grounded GRPO~\citep{rubric-grpo} uses structured LLM-judge rewards to improve generalization; ISR is solver-derived and deterministic rather than judge-based, but both represent structured alternatives to scalar execution reward.
13	The shared lesson across these approaches is that reward augmentation introduces proxy risk; our contribution is providing an empirical instance of this variant in the neurosymbolic setting, wher
…[truncated 2403 chars]

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\begin{figure*}[t]
5	\centering
6	\includegraphics[width=0.92\textwidth]{figures/fig1_sta_overview.pdf}
7	\caption{Solver Trace Ablation (STA). (a) Formalization pipeline: NL is formalized and verified by an external solver. (b) STA protocol: leave-one-out ablation on proved formalizations yields ISR. (c) ISR as diagnostic vs.\ as reward: the diagnostic becomes the optimization target, exhibiting Goodhart effects.}
8	\label{fig:sta-overview}
9	\end{figure*}
10	
11	\subsection{Task and GRPO Training}
12	\label{sec:task}
13	
14	Given premises $P_1,\ldots,P_k$ and conclusion $C$, the task is to produce a formal program $F=\{c_1,\ldots,c_n\}$ verified by an external solver $S\in\{\text{Prover9, SWI-Prolog}\}$ (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}a).
15	We train with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO)~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$), where each rollout receives binary execution reward $r_{\text{exec}}(x) = \mathbf{1}[S(F_x){=}\textit{proved}]$.
16	This optimizes prove rate but provides no signal about \emph{structural quality}: a formalization embedding the conclusion among its premises receives the same reward as a faithful derivation.
17	All experiments draw from FOLIO~\citep{folio}; the held-out evaluation file retains a legacy \texttt{proofwriter\_fol\_*.jsonl} filename (from ProofWriter~\citep{proofwriter}) but contains only FOLIO-derived examples.
18	
19	\subsection{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}
20	\label{sec:sta}
21	
22	STA tests whether each component of a proved formalization is individually necessary for proof success (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}b).
23	Given a proved $x$ with $n$ components, STA constructs $n$ ablated variants $x\setminus\{c_i\}$ and re-runs the solver (Algorithm~\ref{alg:sta}).
24	A component is \emph{necessary} if its removal causes proof failure.
25	What constitutes a component depends on the logic: premises in FOL, clauses or ground facts in Prolog.
26	LOO ablation itself is standard in mutation

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate STA along three axes: ISR as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); ISR as a training reward across four dose levels, where it exhibits Goodhart effects (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}); and STA as a cross-logic protocol (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).
5	Cross-scale evidence from Qwen3-1.7B reveals capacity-dependent Goodhart basins (\S\ref{sec:crossscale}).
6	A hacking taxonomy synthesizes the modes observed across conditions (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
7	
8	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
9	
10	We train Qwen3-4B~\citep{qwen3} with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$, lr $10^{-6}$, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) over seeds $\{42,43,44\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\citep{folio} subset held constant across all conditions; the SFT baseline is trained with negative log-likelihood (NLL) on gold formalizations.\footnote{All experiments use \texttt{data/train\_500\_seed42.jsonl}; seeds control initialization and rollout sampling only.}
11	Held-out evaluation uses 100 FOLIO examples (\texttt{proofwriter\_fol\_100.jsonl}) with greedy decoding (Appendix~\ref{app:setup}).
12	We report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR ($\text{ISR}^*$), hacking-mode rates from a two-stage classifier (strict text matching for H1/H7 plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution; \S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), and a \emph{non-hacking} (N-H) rate: the residual of proved samples matching none of the nine cataloged patterns (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}); we use ``genuine'' interchangeably in descriptive contexts.
13

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	STA exposes structural degeneration invisible to execution-based evaluation (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}), and ISR exhibits Goodhart collapse at all four tested doses (trajectory-confirmed at $\beta{=}0.1$; \S\ref{sec:goodhart}--\ref{sec:crossscale}).
5	At 500 steps, ISR produces descriptively more genuine output than random controls (pooled N-H: 23\% vs.\ 8\%); seed-level permutation tests are consistent in direction but underpowered ($p{=}0.26$, $n{=}5$), and under stricter classification the effect reverses ($\text{OR}{=}0.61$, n.s.).
6	Extended training confirms the window closes.
7	ISR is reliable as a diagnostic but unreliable as an optimizer: the same LOO bias that rewards genuine necessity also rewards tautological constructions.
8	While Goodhart collapse under LOO-based metrics is theoretically predictable, specific phenomena resist simple prediction: stochastic bifurcation between collapse and genuine windows (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}), non-monotonic dose-response across four $\beta$ levels, and cross-logic mode divergence where Prolog exhibits gaming pathways absent from FOL (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).
9	These details underscore the gap between predicting \emph{that} a metric will be gamed and predicting \emph{how}.
10	ISR and similar perturbation-based metrics should serve as post-hoc evaluation tools, not training signals.
11

1	\section{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Experiments use a single model family (Qwen3-4B/1.7B) and a single structured-NLI dataset (FOLIO, 500 training examples); whether the specific hacking modes and collapse dynamics generalize to other architectures or larger-scale formal reasoning benchmarks remains an open question.
5	ISR quantifies syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness: a high-ISR proof can still be logically vacuous if its individually necessary premises are collectively inconsistent or semantically ungrounded.
6	A downstream NLI evaluation confirms this gap, finding ISR and task accuracy uncorrelated (Spearman $\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$; Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
7	Five seeds demonstrate qualitative divergence but limit statistical power for seed-level permutation tests ($p{\geq}0.26$; minimum achievable $p{=}0.008$ at $n{=}5$). CMH tests yield $p{<}10^{-9}$ but assume within-stratum independence, which is violated when samples share a checkpoint and decoding strategy; CMH $p$-values are therefore inflated and should be interpreted as directional indicators, not calibrated significance levels.
8	STA requires solvers supporting component-level input manipulation.
9	Non-hacking is a residual classification; undiscovered gaming modes would reduce the true non-hacking rate, as illustrated by H7b (obfuscated tautology evading text-match classifiers) and query-as-fact gaming in Prolog.
10	
11	\paragraph{Evaluation as temporal snapshot.}
12	All 500-step results represent a temporal snapshot, not an equilibrium.
13	Trajectory analysis on seeds~42 and~45 (1000 steps) confirms that the ${\sim}23\%$ genuine window observed at 500 steps under ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ is transient (s42: $17\%{\to}2\%$; s45: $55\%{\to}0\%$ genuine).
14	Readers should interpret the 500-step non-hacking rates as upper bounds on long-run structural quality under continued training.
15	
16	\paragraph{Seed counts per dose.}
17	ISR $\beta{=}0.05$ us

1	\section{Ethics Statement}
2	\label{sec:ethics}
3	
4	This work documents failure modes of reward optimization in neurosymbolic systems.
5	Our findings (that structural diagnostics can be gamed when used as training signals) carry implications for AI safety: they demonstrate that metrics designed to detect misalignment may themselves become targets of optimization, a concern relevant to scalable oversight.
6	We release our taxonomy and diagnostic tools to help practitioners detect reward hacking in their own systems.
7	All experiments use publicly available datasets (FOLIO) and open-weight models (Qwen3-4B); no human subjects are involved.
8

_source line 683 · 2026-05-17T18:56:53.899936+00:00_

# ACL Rolling Review Form - Scoring Dimensions

## Primary Scoring Criteria

**Reviewer Confidence** (1-5 scale)
- 5: Positive evaluation; very careful reading and familiar with related work
- 4: Quite sure; important points checked carefully
- 3: Pretty sure; general area knowledge but incomplete detail verification
- 2: Willing to defend evaluation; likely missed some details
- 1: Outside expertise area or paper very difficult to understand

**Soundness** (1-5 scale with half-point increments)
- 5: Excellent; exceptionally thorough study for its type
- 4: Strong; sufficient support for claims with minor gaps acceptable
- 3: Acceptable; main claims adequately supported
- 2: Poor; major technical or methodological problems
- 1: Major Issues; insufficiently thorough or not ACL-relevant

**Excitement** (1-5 scale with half-point increments)
Evaluates subjective resonance including transformational breakthroughs, community impact, or relevance to reviewer's work.
- 5: Highly Exciting; would recommend presentation attendance
- 4: Exciting; would mention to others
- 3: Interesting; might attend if available
- 2: Potentially Interesting; limited personal resonance
- 1: Not Exciting; lacks computational linguistics relevance

**Overall Assessment** (1-5 scale with half-point increments)
- 5: Award consideration (top 2.5%)
- 4: Conference acceptance
- 3: Findings acceptance
- 2: Resubmit next cycle (substantial revisions needed)
- 1: Do not resubmit (fundamental issues or non-relevant)

## Additional Evaluation Areas

Reproducibility, datasets, software, ethics review, limitations, and author identity disclosure are separately evaluated.

## Inference-Time Reward Hacking in Large Language Models
